She finally sought help after a supervisor commented on her reduced engagement and a close friend from church pointed out that she hadn’t seemed like her usual upbeat, competitive self for a long time, which made her realize how long she had been feeling this way and prompted her to ask her campus health clinic for a mental health referral.\n- Chief Complaint: My sleep has been messed up for a long time, and no matter how I change my schedule I can’t seem to get any real rest.\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): For a long time now, even on days when nothing actually goes wrong, I just feel this low, gray heaviness in the background, like I’m going through the motions at work and school without ever really feeling excited or genuinely happy about anything anymore.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): I used to be all about grabbing food with my teammates or trying new places after class, but now most days I’m just not hungry, I kind of pick at whatever’s around or skip meals on shift and only realize later that I barely ate all day.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): With the rotating shifts my sleep is all over the place, but what really gets me is that even on nights when I’m actually home and in bed on time, I lie there for an hour or more with my mind circling around work quotas and homework, then wake up a couple times in the middle of the night and never feel like I got real rest.\n- S029 (Fatigue): I feel tired pretty much all the time, like even simple stuff—doing a load of laundry, going to open gym, or staying focused in class after a shift—takes way more effort than it used to, and no amount of sleeping in on my days off seems to really recharge me.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Because I’m always dragging and down, I’ve started turning in assignments late, calling out or swapping shifts more often, and I barely see my friends or go to practices anymore, which is starting to worry my supervisor During the day, I don't really have those active racing thoughts; it's more of a low, heavy feeling and just being constantly exhausted.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge during the day, even when you're not thinking about your work or school responsibilities, or is your anxiety mainly tied to those specific stressors at night?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really feel anxious or on edge during the day. Instead, it's just this low, gray heaviness where I'm exhausted and going through the motions. The anxious, racing thoughts are almost entirely at night when I'm trying to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt a significant improvement in your mood and energy levels, even if briefly, over the past few months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had any times where my mood or energy improved. This low, gray feeling and constant fatigue have been pretty steady and continuous for over two years now. Even on my days off, I just can't seem to recharge.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're still able to enjoy activities or hobbies that you used to like, or has your interest in these things decreased over time?\n\nPatient: My interest has definitely decreased. I used to love playing in my basketball league, going dancing, and volunteering for STEM, but now I barely ever go to practices or events. It's just really hard to feel genuinely excited or happy about any of those things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by 'hard to feel genuinely excited or happy' about activities you used to enjoy, is it that you no longer find pleasure in them or is it more that you just don't have the energy to participate?\n\nPatient: It’s actually a combination of both. I have this constant low, gray heaviness where I just don't feel that spark of excitement or genuine happiness for things I used to love, like basketball, dancing, or volunteering. At the same time, I'm so physically and mentally exhausted all the time that even trying to participate feels like it takes way too much effort.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned a decrease in enjoying activities you used to like, can you tell me if there are any activities at all that still bring you some pleasure or make you feel slightly better, even if it's fleeting?\n\nPatient: No, unfortunately nothing really seems to bring me pleasure or make me feel better, even temporarily. The things I used to love—like my basketball league, dancing, or volunteering—now just feel like way too much effort and don't spark any joy or relief, leaving me feeling completely flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a decrease in pleasure from activities that you normally would have enjoyed, consistently over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've definitely lost interest and stopped participating in things I used to love, like my basketball league, dancing, and STEM volunteering, because they just don't make me happy anymore. But this isn't just from the past two weeks—it's been a steady, long-term change that I've been feeling for over two years now.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned feeling this way for over two years, can you tell me if there have been any distinct periods within that time where your symptoms were significantly worse or better, or has it been a relatively consistent low mood and energy level?"
  }

I just feel like I'm constantly tossing and turning, and I'm really hoping to get some help with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your sleep issues are affecting your daily activities, such as work, concentration, or mood, or are they primarily bothering you at night?\n\nPatient: It definitely affects both; at night, my mind just races with worries about my work quotas and schoolwork, keeping me awake and waking me up throughout the night. During the day, I'm constantly exhausted and in a low, heavy mood, which has led to me turning in homework late, swapping shifts, and skipping out on seeing my friends.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the worries and racing thoughts that keep you awake at night, are they primarily focused on specific stressors or are they more general and intrusive thoughts?\n\nPatient: They are definitely very specific. I just lie there and my mind constantly circles around my work quotas and whether I'm going to get my school assignments done on time. It's not really random or general thoughts, just the constant pressure of trying to keep up with both.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these worries and racing thoughts during the day as well, or are they mostly limited to when you're trying to fall asleep or stay asleep?\n\nPatient: It's mostly at night when I'm lying in bed trying to fall asleep that my mind starts circling like that. During the day, I don't really have those active racing thoughts; it's more of a low, heavy feeling and just being constantly exhausted.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge during the day, even when you're not thinking about your work or school responsibilities, or is your anxiety mainly tied to those specific stressors at night?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really feel anxious or on edge during the day. Instead, it's just this low, gray heaviness where I'm exhausted and going through the motions. The anxious, racing thoughts are almost entirely at night when I'm trying to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt a significant improvement in your mood and energy levels, even if briefly, over the past few months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had any times where my mood or energy improved. This low, gray feeling and constant fatigue have been pretty steady and continuous for over two years now. Even on my days off, I just can't seem to recharge.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're still able to enjoy activities or hobbies that you used to like, or has your interest in these things decreased over time?\n\nPatient: My interest has definitely decreased. I used to love playing in my basketball league, going dancing, and volunteering for STEM, but now I barely ever go to practices or events. It's just really hard to feel genuinely excited or happy about any of those things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by 'hard to feel genuinely excited or happy' about activities you used to enjoy, is it that you no longer find pleasure in them or is it more that you just don't have the energy to participate?\n\nPatient: It’s actually a combination of both. I have this constant low, gray heaviness where I just don't feel that spark of excitement or genuine happiness for things I used to love, like basketball, dancing, or volunteering. At the same time, I'm so physically and mentally exhausted all the time that even trying to participate feels like it takes way too much effort.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned a decrease in enjoying activities you used to like, can you tell me if there are any activities at all that still bring you some pleasure or make you feel slightly better, even if it's fleeting?\n\nPatient: No, unfortunately nothing really seems to bring me pleasure or make me feel better, even temporarily. The things I used to love—like my basketball league, dancing, or volunteering—now just feel like way too much effort and don't spark any joy or relief, leaving me feeling completely flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a decrease in pleasure from activities that you normally would have enjoyed, consistently over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've definitely lost interest and stopped participating in things I used to love, like my basketball league, dancing, and STEM volunteering, because they just don't make me happy anymore. But this isn't just from the past two weeks—it's been a steady, long-term change that I've been feeling for over two years now.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned feeling this way for over two years, can you tell me if there have been any distinct periods within that time where your symptoms were significantly worse or better, or has it been a relatively consistent low mood and energy level?\n\nPatient: It has been very consistent and steady, with no real waves of feeling better or worse. This low, gray heaviness has just become my constant new normal, and I've felt this exact same way almost every single day for the last two years.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling this way for over two years, but I'd like to clarify if there was a specific point or event that started this period of low mood and energy, or if it gradually developed over time?\n\nPatient: It definitely developed gradually rather than from one sudden event. It started about two and a half years ago when I took on a demanding QA inspector job with rotating shifts while still taking classes part-time. I was excited at first, but the irregular hours and pressure slowly wore me down over a few months until this low, tired feeling became my new normal.\n\nDoctor: You've described a gradual onset of symptoms over a few months, but can you clarify if there was ever a period, even briefly, within the last two years where your symptoms seemed to lift or improve for more than a couple of weeks, which might help differentiate between a persistent depressive disorder and a major depressive episode?\n\nPatient: No, there really hasn't been any period where the symptoms lifted. This low, worn-down feeling has been completely steady and constant over the last two years, without any breaks or times where I felt back to my normal self for more than just a few days at most."

During the day, I don't really have those active racing thoughts; it's more of a low, heavy feeling and just being constantly exhausted.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge during the day, even when you're not thinking about your work or school responsibilities, or is your anxiety mainly tied to those specific stressors at night?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really feel anxious or on edge during the day. Instead, it's just this low, gray heaviness where I'm exhausted and going through the motions. The anxious, racing thoughts are almost entirely at night when I'm trying to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt a significant improvement in your mood and energy levels, even if briefly, over the past few months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had any times where my mood or energy improved. This low, gray feeling and constant fatigue have been pretty steady and continuous for over two years now. Even on my days off, I just can't seem to recharge.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're still able to enjoy activities or hobbies that you used to like, or has your interest in these things decreased over time?\n\nPatient: My interest has definitely decreased. I used to love playing in my basketball league, going dancing, and volunteering for STEM, but now I barely ever go to practices or events. It's just really hard to feel genuinely excited or happy about any of those things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by 'hard to feel genuinely excited or happy' about activities you used to enjoy, is it that you no longer find pleasure in them or is it more that you just don't have the energy to participate?\n\nPatient: It’s actually a combination of both. I have this constant low, gray heaviness where I just don't feel that spark of excitement or genuine happiness for things I used to love, like basketball, dancing, or volunteering. At the same time, I'm so physically and mentally exhausted all the time that even trying to participate feels like it takes way too much effort.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned a decrease in enjoying activities you used to like, can you tell me if there are any activities at all that still bring you some pleasure or make you feel slightly better, even if it's fleeting?\n\nPatient: No, unfortunately nothing really seems to bring me pleasure or make me feel better, even temporarily. The things I used to love—like my basketball league, dancing, or volunteering—now just feel like way too much effort and don't spark any joy or relief, leaving me feeling completely flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a decrease in pleasure from activities that you normally would have enjoyed, consistently over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've definitely lost interest and stopped participating in things I used to love, like my basketball league, dancing, and STEM volunteering, because they just don't make me happy anymore. But this isn't just from the past two weeks—it's been a steady, long-term change that I've been feeling for over two years now.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned feeling this way for over two years, can you tell me if there have been any distinct periods within that time where your symptoms were significantly worse or better, or has it been a relatively consistent low mood and energy level?\n\nPatient: It has been very consistent and steady, with no real waves of feeling better or worse. This low, gray heaviness has just become my constant new normal, and I've felt this exact same way almost every single day for the last two years.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling this way for over two years, but I'd like to clarify if there was a specific point or event that started this period of low mood and energy, or if it gradually developed over time?\n\nPatient: It definitely developed gradually rather than from one sudden event. It started about two and a half years ago when I took on a demanding QA inspector job with rotating shifts while still taking classes part-time. I was excited at first, but the irregular hours and pressure slowly wore me down over a few months until this low, tired feeling became my new normal.\n\nDoctor: You've described a gradual onset of symptoms over a few months, but can you clarify if there was ever a period, even briefly, within the last two years where your symptoms seemed to lift or improve for more than a couple of weeks, which might help differentiate between a persistent depressive disorder and a major depressive episode?\n\nPatient: No, there really hasn't been any period where the symptoms lifted. This low, worn-down feeling has been completely steady and constant over the last two years, without any breaks or times where I felt back to my normal self for more than just a few days at most.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned a constant low mood and energy level for over two years without significant improvement, can you confirm if you've had any periods of at least two weeks where your symptoms were significantly worse, meeting the criteria for a major depressive episode within this timeframe?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, but I haven't noticed any specific two-week periods where things got significantly worse. This low and tired feeling has just been pretty steady and constant for the past two years without any real dips or spikes."

I just feel like I'm constantly tossing and turning, and I'm really hoping to get some help with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your sleep issues are affecting your daily activities, such as work, concentration, or mood, or are they primarily bothering you at night?\n\nPatient: It definitely affects both; at night, my mind just races with worries about my work quotas and schoolwork, keeping me awake and waking me up throughout the night. During the day, I'm constantly exhausted and in a low, heavy mood, which has led to me turning in homework late, swapping shifts, and skipping out on seeing my friends.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the worries and racing thoughts that keep you awake at night, are they primarily focused on specific stressors or are they more general and intrusive thoughts?\n\nPatient: They are definitely very specific. I just lie there and my mind constantly circles around my work quotas and whether I'm going to get my school assignments done on time. It's not really random or general thoughts, just the constant pressure of trying to keep up with both.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these worries and racing thoughts during the day as well, or are they mostly limited to when you're trying to fall asleep or stay asleep?\n\nPatient: It's mostly at night when I'm lying in bed trying to fall asleep that my mind starts circling like that. During the day, I don't really have those active racing thoughts; it's more of a low, heavy feeling and just being constantly exhausted.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge during the day, even when you're not thinking about your work or school responsibilities, or is your anxiety mainly tied to those specific stressors at night?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really feel anxious or on edge during the day. Instead, it's just this low, gray heaviness where I'm exhausted and going through the motions. The anxious, racing thoughts are almost entirely at night when I'm trying to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt a significant improvement in your mood and energy levels, even if briefly, over the past few months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had any times where my mood or energy improved. This low, gray feeling and constant fatigue have been pretty steady and continuous for over two years now. Even on my days off, I just can't seem to recharge.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're still able to enjoy activities or hobbies that you used to like, or has your interest in these things decreased over time?\n\nPatient: My interest has definitely decreased. I used to love playing in my basketball league, going dancing, and volunteering for STEM, but now I barely ever go to practices or events. It's just really hard to feel genuinely excited or happy about any of those things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by 'hard to feel genuinely excited or happy' about activities you used to enjoy, is it that you no longer find pleasure in them or is it more that you just don't have the energy to participate?\n\nPatient: It’s actually a combination of both. I have this constant low, gray heaviness where I just don't feel that spark of excitement or genuine happiness for things I used to love, like basketball, dancing, or volunteering. At the same time, I'm so physically and mentally exhausted all the time that even trying to participate feels like it takes way too much effort.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned a decrease in enjoying activities you used to like, can you tell me if there are any activities at all that still bring you some pleasure or make you feel slightly better, even if it's fleeting?\n\nPatient: No, unfortunately nothing really seems to bring me pleasure or make me feel better, even temporarily. The things I used to love—like my basketball league, dancing, or volunteering—now just feel like way too much effort and don't spark any joy or relief, leaving me feeling completely flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a decrease in pleasure from activities that you normally would have enjoyed, consistently over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've definitely lost interest and stopped participating in things I used to love, like my basketball league, dancing, and STEM volunteering, because they just don't make me happy anymore. But this isn't just from the past two weeks—it's been a steady, long-term change that I've been feeling for over two years now.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned feeling this way for over two years, can you tell me if there have been any distinct periods within that time where your symptoms were significantly worse or better, or has it been a relatively consistent low mood and energy level?\n\nPatient: It has been very consistent and steady, with no real waves of feeling better or worse. This low, gray heaviness has just become my constant new normal, and I've felt this exact same way almost every single day for the last two years.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling this way for over two years, but I'd like to clarify if there was a specific point or event that started this period of low mood and energy, or if it gradually developed over time?\n\nPatient: It definitely developed gradually rather than from one sudden event. It started about two and a half years ago when I took on a demanding QA inspector job with rotating shifts while still taking classes part-time. I was excited at first, but the irregular hours and pressure slowly wore me down over a few months until this low, tired feeling became my new normal.\n\nDoctor: You've described a gradual onset of symptoms over a few months, but can you clarify if there was ever a period, even briefly, within the last two years where your symptoms seemed to lift or improve for more than a couple of weeks, which might help differentiate between a persistent depressive disorder and a major depressive episode?\n\nPatient: No, there really hasn't been any period where the symptoms lifted. This low, worn-down feeling has been completely steady and constant over the last two years, without any breaks or times where I felt back to my normal self for more than just a few days at most.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned a constant low mood and energy level for over two years without significant improvement, can you confirm if you've had any periods of at least two weeks where your symptoms were significantly worse, meeting the criteria for a major depressive episode within this timeframe?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, but I haven't noticed any specific two-week periods where things got significantly worse. This low and tired feeling has just been pretty steady and constant for the past two years without any real dips or spikes.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any two-week period over the last two years where you felt significantly more down, hopeless, or had more trouble concentrating than your usual low mood, even if it was just a slight worsening of your symptoms?\n\nPatient: I really can't recall any specific two-week period where things felt significantly worse or different than usual. It has just been a steady, constant low and gray feeling that has remained pretty much the same over the last two years."

It has just been a steady, constant low and gray feeling that has remained pretty much the same over the last two years.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back over the last two years and try to recall if there was ever a two-week period where you experienced at least five symptoms of depression, such as depressed mood, loss of interest, changes in appetite or sleep, fatigue, feelings of worthlessness, or difficulty concentrating, and if so, did these symptoms represent a noticeable worsening from your usual state?"
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I'm not really sure, and I haven't noticed any specific two-week period where things suddenly got much worse with that many symptoms at once. It has really just felt like a constant, steady low background feeling for the entire two years.

I used to love playing in my basketball league, going dancing, and volunteering for STEM, but now I barely ever go to practices or events. It's just really hard to feel genuinely excited or happy about any of those things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by 'hard to feel genuinely excited or happy' about activities you used to enjoy, is it that you no longer find pleasure in them or is it more that you just don't have the energy to participate?\n\nPatient: It’s actually a combination of both. I have this constant low, gray heaviness where I just don't feel that spark of excitement or genuine happiness for things I used to love, like basketball, dancing, or volunteering. At the same time, I'm so physically and mentally exhausted all the time that even trying to participate feels like it takes way too much effort.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned a decrease in enjoying activities you used to like, can you tell me if there are any activities at all that still bring you some pleasure or make you feel slightly better, even if it's fleeting?\n\nPatient: No, unfortunately nothing really seems to bring me pleasure or make me feel better, even temporarily. The things I used to love—like my basketball league, dancing, or volunteering—now just feel like way too much effort and don't spark any joy or relief, leaving me feeling completely flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a decrease in pleasure from activities that you normally would have enjoyed, consistently over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've definitely lost interest and stopped participating in things I used to love, like my basketball league, dancing, and STEM volunteering, because they just don't make me happy anymore. But this isn't just from the past two weeks—it's been a steady, long-term change that I've been feeling for over two years now.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned feeling this way for over two years, can you tell me if there have been any distinct periods within that time where your symptoms were significantly worse or better, or has it been a relatively consistent low mood and energy level?\n\nPatient: It has been very consistent and steady, with no real waves of feeling better or worse. This low, gray heaviness has just become my constant new normal, and I've felt this exact same way almost every single day for the last two years.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling this way for over two years, but I'd like to clarify if there was a specific point or event that started this period of low mood and energy, or if it gradually developed over time?\n\nPatient: It definitely developed gradually rather than from one sudden event. It started about two and a half years ago when I took on a demanding QA inspector job with rotating shifts while still taking classes part-time. I was excited at first, but the irregular hours and pressure slowly wore me down over a few months until this low, tired feeling became my new normal.\n\nDoctor: You've described a gradual onset of symptoms over a few months, but can you clarify if there was ever a period, even briefly, within the last two years where your symptoms seemed to lift or improve for more than a couple of weeks, which might help differentiate between a persistent depressive disorder and a major depressive episode?\n\nPatient: No, there really hasn't been any period where the symptoms lifted. This low, worn-down feeling has been completely steady and constant over the last two years, without any breaks or times where I felt back to my normal self for more than just a few days at most.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned a constant low mood and energy level for over two years without significant improvement, can you confirm if you've had any periods of at least two weeks where your symptoms were significantly worse, meeting the criteria for a major depressive episode within this timeframe?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, but I haven't noticed any specific two-week periods where things got significantly worse. This low and tired feeling has just been pretty steady and constant for the past two years without any real dips or spikes.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any two-week period over the last two years where you felt significantly more down, hopeless, or had more trouble concentrating than your usual low mood, even if it was just a slight worsening of your symptoms?\n\nPatient: I really can't recall any specific two-week period where things felt significantly worse or different than usual. It has just been a steady, constant low and gray feeling that has remained pretty much the same over the last two years.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back over the last two years and try to recall if there was ever a two-week period where you experienced at least five symptoms of depression, such as depressed mood, loss of interest, changes in appetite or sleep, fatigue, feelings of worthlessness, or difficulty concentrating, and if so, did these symptoms represent a noticeable worsening from your usual state?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, and I haven't noticed any specific two-week period where things suddenly got much worse with that many symptoms at once. It has really just felt like a constant, steady low background feeling for the entire two years.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any instance, even briefly, where you felt like you were back to your normal self or had a significant improvement in your mood for at least two weeks, which might help clarify whether you've had a major depressive episode within this persistent low mood?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I definitely haven't had any periods like that. Over the last two years, this low, heavy, and exhausted feeling has been completely constant, without any breaks where I felt like my old self for two weeks or more. It has just been one steady, unchanging stretch of feeling worn down.
